    37	  smokeOutputDigest: 'out-123',
    38	  gitHead: 'deadbeef',
    39	  gitContentDigest: 'content-999',
    40	  childAssignment: 'implementer=codex/gpt-5.6-sol',
    41	});
    42	
    43	// Each field -> a permissive needle the drift name must satisfy.
    44	const FIELD_NEEDLE = {
    45	  stateDigest: /state/i,
    46	  smokeCmd: /cmd|command/i,
    47	  smokeOutputDigest: /output/i,
    48	  gitHead: /head/i,
    49	  gitContentDigest: /content|git/i,
    50	  childAssignment: /child|assignment/i,
    51	};
    52	
    53	// ===========================================================================
    54	describe('smokeApprovalToken — deterministic digest over the bound inputs', () => {
    55	  it('same inputs => same token', () => {
    56	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();
    57	    assert.equal(smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS()), smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS()), 'the token is a pure function of its inputs');
    58	  });
    59	
    60	  it('ANY single input change => a different token', () => {
    61	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();
    62	    const baseline = smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS());
    63	    for (const field of Object.keys(INPUTS())) {
    64	      const changed = { ...INPUTS(), [field]: `${INPUTS()[field]}-MOVED` };
    65	      assert.notEqual(smokeApprovalToken(changed), baseline, `changing ${field} must change the token`);
    66	    }
    67	  });
    68	
    69	  it('field boundaries are separated — a shifted split across two fields yields a DIFFERENT token (finding 3)', () => {
    70	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();
    71	    // Same six fields, but the boundary between stateDigest and smokeCmd moves:
    72	    // a separator-less concatenation would collide ('xyz' either way).
    73	    const a = smokeApprovalToken({ ...INPUTS(), stateDigest: 'x', smokeCmd: 'yz' });
    74	    const b = smokeApprovalToken({ ...INPUTS(), stateDigest: 'xy', smokeCmd: 'z' });
    75	    assert.notEqual(a, b, 'per-field boundaries must be preserved (no separator-less concatenation collision)');
    76	  });
    77	});
    78	
    79	// ===========================================================================
    80	describe('smokeApprovalToken / verifySmokeToken — partial inputs (finding 4)', () => {
    81	  it('a missing bound field is rejected — the token cannot be computed from partial inputs', () => {
    82	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();
    83	    const partial = INPUTS();
    84	    delete partial.gitHead; // a required bound input is absent
    85	    assert.throws(() => smokeApprovalToken(partial), /gitHead|missing|required/i, 'computing a token over partial inputs must throw naming the gap');
    86	  });
    87	
    88	  it('verify is NEVER ok when the current inputs are partial (cannot re-derive)', () => {
    89	    const { smokeApprovalToken, verifySmokeToken } = M();
    90	    const token = smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS());
    91	    const partial = INPUTS();
    92	    delete partial.smokeOutputDigest;
    93	    let r;
    94	    assert.doesNotThrow(() => { r = verifySmokeToken(token, partial); }, 'verify must degrade gracefully, not throw, on partial inputs');
    95	    assert.equal(r.ok, false, 'a token can never verify ok against partial current inputs');
    96	  });
    97	});
    98	
    99	// ===========================================================================
   100	describe('verifySmokeToken — recompute and name the drifted input(s)', () => {
   101	  it('a token verifies ok against its own inputs', () => {
   102	    const { smokeApprovalToken, verifySmokeToken } = M();
   103	    const token = smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS());
   104	    const r = verifySmokeToken(token, INPUTS());

   117	    }
   118	  });

   260	   219	  });

   326	    45	  stateDigest: /state/i,
   327	    46	  smokeCmd: /cmd|command/i,
   328	    47	  smokeOutputDigest: /output/i,

   336	    55	  it('same inputs => same token', () => {
   337	    56	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();
   338	    57	    assert.equal(smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS()), smokeApprovalToken(INPUTS()), 'the token is a pure function of its inputs');
   339	    58	  });
   340	    59	
   341	    60	  it('ANY single input change => a different token', () => {
   342	    61	    const { smokeApprovalToken } = M();

   344	    63	    for (const field of Object.keys(INPUTS())) {
   345	    64	      const changed = { ...INPUTS(), [field]: `${INPUTS()[field]}-MOVED` };
   346	    65	      assert.notEqual(smokeApprovalToken(changed), baseline, `changing ${field} must change the token`);
   347	    66	    }
   348	    67	  });
   349	    68	});
   350	    69

   356	    75	    const r = verifySmokeToken(token, INPUTS());

   386	     1	# Goal-loop + dual-worktree pipeline (Layers 2+3, v1 plan)
   387	     2	
   388	     3	Status: APPROVED — Gate 1 passed at iteration 4 of 5 (2026-07-18,
   389	     4	codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh, `degraded: model-fallback`).
   390	     5	
   391	     6	## Context
   392	     7	
   393	     8	baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2
   394	     9	passed, a 40-finding surface audit folded, and the live e2e handoff loop
   395	    10	verified through the real installed plugin. Layers 2 and 3 automate the
   396	    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
   397	    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
   398	    13	pipeline (Layer 3).
   399	    14	
   400	    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
   401	    16	
   402	    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
   403	    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
   404	    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
   405	    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
   406	    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
   407	    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
   408	    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
   409	    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
   410	    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
   411	    26	   every gate.
   412	    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
   413	    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
   414	    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
   415	    30	
   416	    31	## Design
   417	    32	
   418	    33	### Loop spec
   419	    34	
   420	    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
   421	    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
   422	    37	
   423	    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
   424	    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
   425	    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
   426	    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
   427	    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
   428	    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
   429	    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
   430	    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
   431	    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
   432	    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
   433	    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
   434	    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
   435	    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
   436	    51	  hardcoded models.
   437	    52	
   438	    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
   439	    54	
   440	    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
   441	    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
   442	    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
   443	    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
   444	    59	
   445	    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
   446	    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
   447	    62	  re-check before merge.
   448	    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
   449	    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
   450	    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
   451	    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
   452	    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
   453	    68	
   454	    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
   455	    70	
   456	    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
   457	    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
   458	    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
   459	    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
   460	    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
   461	    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
   462	    77	
   463	    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
   464	    79	
   465	    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
   466	    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
   467	    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
   468	    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
   469	    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
   470	    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
   471	    86	
   472	    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
   473	    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
   474	    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
   475	    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
   476	    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
   477	    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
   478	    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
   479	    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
   480	    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
   481	    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
   482	    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
   483	    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
   484	    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   485	   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   486	   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   487	   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   488	   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   489	   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   490	   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   491	   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   492	   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   493	   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   494	   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   495	   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
   496	   111	    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.
   497	   112	
   498	   113	### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)
   499	   114	
   500	   115	`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:
   501	   116	
   502	   117	- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
   503	   118	  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
   504	   119	  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
   505	   120	  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
   506	   121	- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
   507	   122	  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
   508	   123	  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
   509	   124	- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
   510	   125	  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
   511	   126	  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
   512	   127	  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
   513	   128	  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
   514	   129	  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
   515	   130	- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
   516	   131	  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
   517	   132	  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
   518	   133	- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
   519	   134	  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
   520	   135	  without limit-specific evidence.
   521	   136	- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
   522	   137	  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.
   523	   138	
   524	   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   525	   140	
   526	   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   527	   142	
   528	   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   529	   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   530	   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   531	   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   532	   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   533	   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   534	   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   535	   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   536	   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   537	   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   538	   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   539	   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   540	   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   541	   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   542	   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   543	   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   544	   159	
   545	   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   546	   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   547	   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   548	   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   549	   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   550	   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   551	   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   552	   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   553	   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   554	   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   555	   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   556	   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   557	   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   558	   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
   559	   174	  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
   560	   175	  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
   561	   176	  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
   562	   177	  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
   563	   178	  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
   564	   179	  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
   565	   180	  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path
   566	   181	  receive already supports); (5) refresh probes; (6) `receive --prepare` then
   567	   182	  `--commit` back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake flags, the supervisor session
   568	   183	  hint, and the same explicit root, with NO intervening writes; (7) resolver
   569	   184	  output (with the dead entry avoided) picks the relaunch platform+model;
   570	   185	  (8) relaunch the child with the resume prompt. A stale-token rejection
   571	   186	  triggers ONE automatic re-prepare/commit retry; a second failure parks.
   572	   187	  Between prepare and commit the failover holds the loop transaction lock and
   573	   188	  writes NOTHING — `.handoff/loop/` writes are not token-bound inputs, so
   574	   189	  this is enforced by construction and ASSERTED in tests (fs spy: zero
   575	   190	  `.handoff/loop/` writes inside the window), not assumed. Drift-rejection
   576	   191	  tests inject the inputs the token actually binds: git change and probe
   577	   192	  refresh between prepare and commit → stale-token → one retry path. Plus:
   578	   193	  open-bundle (unsealed) limit death; the full seal→receive→relaunch happy
   579	   194	  path.
   580	   195	- **Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)**: the resolver
   581	   196	  gains entry-level avoidance — `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]` alongside
   582	   197	  the existing platform-level `avoid[]` — and the classifier gains a
   583	   198	  `model-unavailable` signature class (distinct from usage-limit; seeded with
   584	   199	  the verified account-tier rejection string). A model rejected at spawn adds
   585	   200	  its {platform, model} entry to avoidEntries and re-resolves: other models
   586	   201	  on the SAME platform remain selectable. Named test: first chain entry
   587	   202	  rejected as model-unavailable → the next entry on the same platform is
   588	   203	  selected, with no platform-wide avoidance.
   589	   204	  All platforms/entries exhausted → the loop parks with a resume-at hint
   590	   205	  instead of dying.
   591	   206	- **Caps**: every gate loops at most 5 iterations; on exhaustion the loop
   592	   207	  parks and escalates (writes `.handoff/loop/ESCALATION.md`, notifies) — never
   593	   208	  a 6th run.
   594	   209	
   595	   210	### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)
   596	   211	
   597	   212	- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
   598	   213	  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
   599	   214	    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
   600	   215	    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
   601	   216	  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
   602	   217	    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
   603	   218	    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
   604	   219	    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
   605	   220	    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
   606	   221	    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
   607	   222	  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
   608	   223	    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
   609	   224	    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
   610	   225	    fast-forward.
   611	   226	
   612	   227	  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
   613	   228	  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
   614	   229	  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
   615	   230	    touched.
   616	   231	  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
   617	   232	    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
   618	   233	    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
   619	   234	  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
   620	   235	    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
   621	   236	  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
   622	   237	    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
   623	   238	    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
   624	   239	  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
   625	   240	    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;
   626	   241	    teardown uses `git worktree remove` + branch deletion so acceptance
   627	   242	    constraint 5 (no residue) holds.
   628	   243	  - Adversarial tests: stale branch from a previous run, dirty worker,
   629	   244	    main moved mid-subtask, merge conflict, raw-deleted `.worktrees/`,
   630	   245	    untracked `.worktrees/` (missing gitignore line → doctor check).
   631	   246	
   632	   247	  **Attribution enforcement for child commits (Gate-1 iteration 1,
   633	   248	  finding 6)** — the invariant is enforced, not assumed:
   634	   249	  - The supervisor sets `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL`
   635	   250	    (sole author identity) in EVERY child's environment.
   636	   251	  - Every merge is gated on an attribution scan over the exact range
   637	   252	    `main..baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N` — author/committer identity plus the
   638	   253	    forbidden-trailer patterns (the existing doctor patterns, applied to the
   639	   254	    full range, not a last-50 window). A violation blocks the merge and
   640	   255	    parks.
   641	   256	  - The existing doctor 50-commit scan remains the repo-wide backstop.
   642	   257	
   643	   258	### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)
   644	   259	
   645	   260	`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`
   646	   261	(state machine + journal + smoke-approval token) · `children.mjs` (full child
   647	   262	lifecycle: spawn/process-group/timeout/log-cap, region-bounded verdict parse,
   648	   263	detect-on-exit) · `failover.mjs` (the limit-death transaction) ·
   649	   264	`worktrees.mjs` (guarded git transactions, branch discipline, attribution
   650	   265	range scan) · `commands/loop.mjs`, `commands/pipeline.mjs`. Plus three Layer-1
   651	   266	extensions: `roles/resolve.mjs` gains `avoidEntries[]` (entry-level
   652	   267	avoidance, backward-compatible — omitted means current behavior); the
   653	   268	signature table gains the `model-unavailable` class; `checkpoint` gains a
   654	   269	`--session <hint>` flag (supervisor identity), and the
   655	   270	`BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` no-op guard lands in the hook entrypoints,
   656	   271	`checkpoint`, AND `session-start` (every hook-invoked command).
   657	   272	
   658	   273	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   659	   274	
   660	   275	1. On a fixture repo, `baton loop run` drives a toy goal end-to-end headlessly:
   661	   276	   plan → gate-1 → smoke slice AS A TDD MINI-CYCLE (failing smoke assertion
   662	   277	   authored and verifier-approved BEFORE the slice implementation — order
   663	   278	   asserted from the journal) → parked for approval → (approved) → 2 subtasks
   664	   279	   TDD → gate-2 → done, with review artifacts under `reviews/` in the fixture.
   665	   280	   Layer-2 children run in the main root with live hooks and neither mutate
   666	   281	   NOR read the supervisor-owned bundle: under `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1`,
   667	   282	   hook commands on all three platforms produce zero bundle writes AND zero
   668	   283	   session-start resume context (both asserted).
   669	   284	2. Simulated limit death (child exits with a verbatim limit banner) mid-subtask
   670	   285	   → the loop seals, remaps the role off the dead platform, resumes on the
   671	   286	   other harness, and completes the subtask. Chained A→B→A tested. Separately:
   672	   287	   a model-unavailable rejection at spawn selects the NEXT entry on the same
   673	   288	   platform (no platform-wide avoidance).
   674	   289	3. `baton pipeline run` completes 2 subtasks with alternating writer/reviewer
   675	   290	   across the two worktrees; the attribution range scan passes for main AND
   676	   291	   both worker branch ranges (child commits included) — sole author, zero AI
   677	   292	   attribution; reviewer children run read-only.
   678	   293	4. The 5-cap parks + escalates on every gate (unit-forced exhaustion test);
   679	   294	   no gate ever runs a 6th iteration. Killing the supervisor (and its
   680	   295	   launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   681	   296	   journal and continues or parks, with no orphaned children.
   682	   297	5. Deleting `.handoff/` and `.worktrees/` leaves the fixture repo exactly as
   683	   298	   a plain git repo (no residue outside gitignored trees).
   684	   299	6. All existing 878 tests stay green; the new suite covers spec validation,
   685	   300	   state-machine transitions (unit, injected io), child verdict parsing
   686	   301	   (fixture transcripts), failover (fake children), and worktree discipline.
   687	   302	
   688	   303	### Top risks
   689	   304	
   690	   305	1. **Headless permission surfaces** — Claude Code children need permission
   691	   306	   flags that stay inside the sandboxed repo; reviewers get read-only
   692	   307	   allowlists. Mitigation: per-role allowlist table + spike task 0.
   693	   308	2. **Verdict-parse drift** — models sometimes wrap the tail; the
   694	   309	   region-bounded parser (Child supervision contract) takes the LAST tail in
   695	   310	   the final-message region only and treats an unparseable one as
   696	   311	   BLOCKED-with-escalation, never as APPROVED.
   697	   312	3. **Cost runaway** — per-phase child caps + iteration caps + the smoke gate
   698	   313	   before any full-run spend; every child's token/duration logged to the loop
   699	   314	   journal.
   700	   315	4. **Worktree state divergence** — only the merger writes main; workers are
   701	   316	   branch-jailed; ff-sync after each merge; `baton pipeline doctor` checks
   702	   317	   worktree health.
   703	   318	5. **Cross-platform child API drift** — the spawn contract lives in ONE module
   704	   319	   (children.mjs) with per-platform fixtures, same seam discipline as
   705	   320	   normalize.mjs.
   706	   321	
   707	   322	## Out of scope for this milestone
   708	   323	
   709	   324	Multi-repo loops; parallel subtask fan-out (>1 writer at once); cloud/cron
   710	   325	driving (the supervisor is local-first; scheduled runs are a later layer);
   711	   326	LLM-judged smoke verdicts (the smoke gate is a human gate by decision 3).
   712	   327

   761	     1	/**
   762	     2	 * Append-only JSONL journal IO, tolerant of the corruption modes a
   763	     3	 * kill-mid-write can produce: a torn (unterminated) tail, a corrupt middle
   764	     4	 * line, and out-of-order sequence numbers.
   765	     5	 */

   767	     7	/**
   768	     8	 * Append one entry as a single JSON line.
   769	     9	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {unknown} obj
   770	    10	 */
   771	    11	export function appendEntry(fs, path, obj) {
   772	    12	  fs.appendFileSync(path, JSON.stringify(obj) + '\n');
   773	    13	}
   774	    14	
   775	    15	/**
   776	    16	 * Read every parseable entry. Never throws.
   777	    17	 * Warnings: {line, kind: 'torn-tail'|'parse-error'|'seq-order', ...}.
   778	    18	 * A non-monotonic numeric seq is flagged but the entry is kept — replay and
   779	    19	 * audit must still see it.
   780	    20	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path
   781	    21	 * @returns {{entries: any[], warnings: any[]}}
   782	    22	 */
   783	    23	export function readAllTolerant(fs, path) {
   784	    24	  /** @type {any[]} */
   785	    25	  const entries = [];
   786	    26	  /** @type {any[]} */
   787	    27	  const warnings = [];
   788	    28	
   789	    29	  let raw;
   790	    30	  try {
   791	    31	    raw = fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   792	    32	  } catch {
   793	    33	    return { entries, warnings };
   794	    34	  }
   795	    35	  if (raw === '') return { entries, warnings };
   796	    36	
   797	    37	  const terminated = raw.endsWith('\n');
   798	    38	  const lines = raw.split('\n');
   799	    39	  if (terminated) lines.pop();
   800	    40	
   801	    41	  let prevSeq = null;
   802	    42	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i += 1) {
   803	    43	    const lineNo = i + 1;
   804	    44	    const isLast = i === lines.length - 1;
   805	    45	    const torn = isLast && !terminated;
   806	    46	    let parsed;
   807	    47	    try {
   808	    48	      parsed = JSON.parse(lines[i]);
   809	    49	    } catch {
   810	    50	      warnings.push(torn ? { line: lineNo, kind: 'torn-tail' } : { line: lineNo, kind: 'parse-error' });
   811	    51	      continue;
   812	    52	    }
   813	    53	    if (typeof parsed?.seq === 'number') {
   814	    54	      if (prevSeq !== null && parsed.seq <= prevSeq) {
   815	    55	        warnings.push({ line: lineNo, kind: 'seq-order', prev: prevSeq, seq: parsed.seq });
   816	    56	      }
   817	    57	      prevSeq = prevSeq === null ? parsed.seq : Math.max(prevSeq, parsed.seq);
   818	    58	    }
   819	    59	    entries.push(parsed);
   820	    60	  }
   821	    61	  return { entries, warnings };
   822	    62	}

   838	    12	/** @param {string} root */
   839	    13	export function bundlePaths(root) {
   840	    14	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
   841	    15	  return {
   842	    16	    dir,
   843	    17	    snapshot: `${dir}/bundle.json`,
   844	    18	    bak: `${dir}/bundle.json.bak`,
   845	    19	    journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson`,
   846	    20	    handoffMd: `${dir}/HANDOFF.md`,
   847	    21	    historyDir: `${dir}/history`,
   848	    22	    lockDir: `${dir}/lock`,
   849	    23	    logDir: `${dir}/log`,
   850	    24	  };
   851	    25	}
   852	    26	
   853	    27	/** @param {string} iso */
   854	    28	const tsForFile = (iso) => iso.replace(/[:.]/g, '-');
   855	    29	
   856	    30	/** @param {ReturnType<typeof bundlePaths>} p */
   857	    31	const markerPath = (p) => `${p.dir}/rotation.marker.json`;
   858	    32	
   859	    33	/**
   860	    34	 * Reconcile a leftover rotation marker (crash recovery, part of load). The
   861	    35	 * mutations run under the repo lock, acquired WITHOUT waiting (gate-2 minor
   862	    36	 * 16): a held lock means someone is mid-operation — reconciliation defers to
   863	    37	 * the next unlocked load instead of writing behind their back.
   864	    38	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof bundlePaths>} p @param {string[]} warnings @param {string} root
   865	    39	 */
   866	    40	function reconcileMarker(io, p, warnings, root) {
   867	    41	  const mp = markerPath(p);
   868	    42	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(mp)) return;
   869	    43	  try {
   870	    44	    withLock(root, { ...io, lockRetry: { attempts: 0, delayMs: 0 } }, () => {
   871	    45	      if (!io.fs.existsSync(mp)) return; // a competitor reconciled while we acquired
   872	    46	      const marker = safeReadJson(io.fs, mp);
   873	    47	      if (!marker.ok) {
   874	    48	        io.fs.unlinkSync(mp);
   875	    49	        warnings.push('rotation marker was unparseable — rolled the rotation back (marker cleared, journal preserved)');
   876	    50	        return;
   877	    51	      }
   878	    52	      const stem = `${tsForFile(marker.value.startedAt)}.${marker.value.kind}`;
   879	    53	      const freeze = `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.json`;
   880	    54	      const rotated = `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.ndjson`;
   881	    55	
   882	    56	      if (io.fs.existsSync(freeze)) {
   883	    57	        // Roll forward: finish whichever step the crash interrupted.
   884	    58	        if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal) && !io.fs.existsSync(rotated)) {
   885	    59	          io.fs.renameSync(p.journal, rotated);
   886	    60	        }
   887	    61	        if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   888	    62	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, p.journal, '');
   889	    63	        }
   890	    64	        io.fs.unlinkSync(mp);
   891	    65	        warnings.push(`completed an interrupted rotation (roll-forward of ${stem}); marker cleared`);
   892	    66	      } else {
   893	    67	        io.fs.unlinkSync(mp);
   894	    68	        warnings.push(`aborted an incomplete rotation (roll-back; ${stem} freeze never landed); marker cleared, journal preserved`);
   895	    69	      }
   896	    70	    });
   897	    71	  } catch (err) {
   898	    72	    if (err instanceof LockHeldError) {
   899	    73	      warnings.push('a rotation marker is present but the repo lock is held — reconciliation deferred to the next unlocked load');
   900	    74	      return;
   901	    75	    }
   902	    76	    throw err;
   903	    77	  }
   904	    78	}
   905	    79	
   906	    80	/**
   907	    81	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof bundlePaths>} p @param {string[]} warnings
   908	    82	 * @returns {any | null} a usable base bundle, or null when nothing exists
   909	    83	 */
   910	    84	function resolveBase(io, p, warnings) {
   911	    85	  const snapshotExists = io.fs.existsSync(p.snapshot);
   912	    86	  if (snapshotExists) {
   913	    87	    const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.snapshot);
   914	    88	    if (snap.ok && validateBundle(snap.value).ok) return snap.value;
   915	    89	    warnings.push(
   916	    90	      snap.ok
   917	    91	        ? 'snapshot bundle.json failed schema validation — attempting recovery'
   918	    92	        : `snapshot bundle.json is unreadable (${snap.error}) — attempting recovery`,
   919	    93	    );
   920	    94	    const bak = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.bak);
   921	    95	    if (bak.ok && validateBundle(bak.value).ok) {
   922	    96	      warnings.push('recovered from backup bundle.json.bak; replaying journal past its journalSeq');
   923	    97	      return bak.value;
   924	    98	    }
   925	    99	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.bak)) warnings.push('backup bundle.json.bak is also unusable');
   926	   100	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   927	   101	      warnings.push('rebuilding the bundle by replaying the whole journal over a fresh seed (recovery of last resort)');
   928	   102	      return emptyBundle({ platform: 'unknown', model: 'unknown', goal: 'unknown (rebuilt from journal)' }, io.now());
   929	   103	    }
   930	   104	    return null;
   931	   105	  }
   932	   106	  if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   933	   107	    warnings.push('no snapshot present — rebuilding the bundle from the journal');
   934	   108	    return emptyBundle({ platform: 'unknown', model: 'unknown', goal: 'unknown (rebuilt from journal)' }, io.now());
   935	   109	  }
   936	   110	  return null;
   937	   111	}
   938	   112	
   939	   113	/**
   940	   114	 * Load the active bundle with full crash recovery. Read path — not lock-gated;
   941	   115	 * rotation-marker reconciliation is part of load. A managed tree that fails
   942	   116	 * the symlink/realpath jail (gate-2 fix 6) is refused outright: bundle null,
   943	   117	 * `unsafe: true`, and the problem in warnings — callers must not treat this as
   944	   118	 * "no bundle yet" and seed through the link.
   945	   119	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   946	   120	 * @returns {{bundle: any | null, warnings: string[], unsafe?: boolean}}
   947	   121	 */
   948	   122	export function loadBundle(root, io) {
   949	   123	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
   950	   124	  /** @type {string[]} */
   951	   125	  const warnings = [];
   952	   126	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.dir)) return { bundle: null, warnings };
   953	   127	
   954	   128	  const safe = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   955	   129	  if (!safe.ok) return { bundle: null, warnings: [safe.problem], unsafe: true };
   956	   130	
   957	   131	  reconcileMarker(io, p, warnings, root);
   958	   132	
   959	   133	  // An interrupted purge is visible on every read path (gate-2 minor 16): the
   960	   134	  // tree may still hold transcript copies until purge-transcript finishes.
   961	   135	  if (io.fs.existsSync(`${p.dir}/purge.marker.json`)) {
   962	   136	    warnings.push('an interrupted purge left its marker — run baton purge-transcript to finish scrubbing every retained copy');
   963	   137	  }
   964	   138	
   965	   139	  let bundle = resolveBase(io, p, warnings);
   966	   140	  if (bundle === null) return { bundle: null, warnings };
   967	   141	
   968	   142	  if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   969	   143	    const { entries, warnings: jw } = readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal);
   970	   144	    for (const w of jw) {
   971	   145	      warnings.push(`journal line ${w.line}: ${w.kind}${w.kind === 'seq-order' ? ` (prev ${w.prev}, got ${w.seq})` : ''}`);
   972	   146	    }
   973	   147	    for (const e of entries) {
   974	   148	      // Envelope guard (gate-2 iter-2 B4): a parseable but non-object line
   975	   149	      // (null, number, bare string) is not an event — skip it with a warning
   976	   150	      // rather than crash replay on `e.seq` / `e.dedupeKey`.
   977	   151	      if (e === null || typeof e !== 'object' || Array.isArray(e)) {
   978	   152	        warnings.push('skipped a malformed journal line (not an event envelope)');
   979	   153	        continue;
   980	   154	      }
   981	   155	      if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq <= bundle.journalSeq) continue;
   982	   156	      bundle = applyEvent(bundle, e);
   983	   157	    }
   984	   158	  }
   985	   159	  return { bundle, warnings };
   986	   160	}
   987	   161	
   988	   162	/**
   989	   163	 * Lock-context snapshot write: caller already holds the repo lock and passes
   990	   164	 * its fencing token; the write is fence-checked immediately before landing
   991	   165	 * (the fast-abort layer — gate-2 fix, reviewer-b finding 12).
   992	   166	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {any} io @param {string} token
   993	   167	 */
   994	   168	export function writeSnapshotIn(root, bundle, io, token) {
   995	   169	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
   996	   170	  return guardedWrite(root, io, token, () => {
   997	   171	    assertHandoffTreeSafe(root, io);
   998	   172	    ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   999	   173	    // Size discipline runs in the write path itself (gate-2 fix 10): every
  1000	   174	    // persisted snapshot is within budget; identity for in-budget bundles.
  1001	   175	    const bounded = compact(bundle);
  1002	   176	    // Validate-before-backup (gate-2 major 15): a corrupt current snapshot
  1003	   177	    // must never overwrite a good .bak — the backup only rotates when the
  1004	   178	    // outgoing snapshot itself would be recoverable.
  1005	   179	    const prev = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.snapshot);
  1006	   180	    if (prev.ok && validateBundle(prev.value).ok) {
  1007	   181	      atomicWriteText(io.fs, p.bak, io.fs.readFileSync(p.snapshot, 'utf8'));
  1008	   182	    }
  1009	   183	    atomicWriteText(io.fs, p.snapshot, JSON.stringify(bounded, null, 2) + '\n');
  1010	   184	    atomicWriteText(io.fs, p.handoffMd, renderHandoffMd(bounded));
  1011	   185	  });
  1012	   186	}
  1013	   187	
  1014	   188	/**
  1015	   189	 * Persist a snapshot: back up the previous good one, write atomically, render
  1016	   190	 * HANDOFF.md alongside. Mutation — runs under the repo lock.
  1017	   191	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {any} io
  1018	   192	 */
  1019	   193	export function writeSnapshot(root, bundle, io) {
  1020	   194	  return withLock(root, io, (token) => writeSnapshotIn(root, bundle, io, token));
  1021	   195	}
  1022	   196	
  1023	   197	/**
  1024	   198	 * Append one journal entry under the repo lock. A pre-numbered entry keeps its
  1025	   199	 * seq; a seq-less entry is allocated max(snapshot.journalSeq, journal tail) + 1.
  1026	   200	 * @param {string} root @param {any} entry @param {any} io
  1027	   201	 * @returns {number} the entry's (possibly allocated) seq
  1028	   202	 */
  1029	   203	export function appendJournal(root, entry, io) {
  1030	   204	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
  1031	   205	  return withLock(root, io, (token) =>
  1032	   206	    guardedWrite(root, io, token, () => {
  1033	   207	      assertHandoffTreeSafe(root, io);
  1034	   208	      ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
  1035	   209	      let seq = entry.seq;
  1036	   210	      let toAppend = entry;
  1037	   211	      if (typeof seq !== 'number') {
  1038	   212	        const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.snapshot);
  1039	   213	        const snapSeq = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.journalSeq === 'number' ? snap.value.journalSeq : 0;
  1040	   214	        let tailSeq = 0;
  1041	   215	        if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
  1042	   216	          for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
  1043	   217	            if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
  1044	   218	          }
  1045	   219	        }
  1046	   220	        seq = Math.max(snapSeq, tailSeq) + 1;
  1047	   221	        toAppend = { ...entry, seq };
  1048	   222	      }
  1049	   223	      appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, toAppend);
  1050	   224	      return seq;
  1051	   225	    }),
  1052	   226	  );
  1053	   227	}
  1054	   228	
  1055	   229	/**
  1056	   230	 * Crash-safe journal rotation (marker → freeze → rename → fresh journal →
  1057	   231	 * clear marker), with 10-per-kind history retention. Mutation — lock-gated.
  1058	   232	 * @param {string} root @param {'finalize'|'takeover'|'receive'} kind @param {any} io
  1059	   233	 * @returns {string} the history stem for this rotation
  1060	   234	 */
  1061	   235	export function rotateJournal(root, kind, io) {
  1062	   236	  return withLock(root, io, (token) => rotateJournalIn(root, kind, io, token));
  1063	   237	}
  1064	   238	
  1065	   239	/**
  1066	   240	 * Lock-context rotation: caller holds the lock and passes its fencing token.
  1067	   241	 * @param {string} root @param {'finalize'|'takeover'|'receive'} kind @param {any} io @param {string} token
  1068	   242	 * @returns {string} the history stem for this rotation
  1069	   243	 */
  1070	   244	export function rotateJournalIn(root, kind, io, token) {
  1071	   245	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
  1072	   246	  return guardedWrite(root, io, token, () => {
  1073	   247	    assertHandoffTreeSafe(root, io);
  1074	   248	    ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
  1075	   249	    const startedAt = io.now();
  1076	   250	    const stem = `${tsForFile(startedAt)}.${kind}`;
  1077	   251	    const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.snapshot);
  1078	   252	    const seq = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.journalSeq === 'number' ? snap.value.journalSeq : 0;
  1079	   253	
  1080	   254	    atomicWriteJson(io.fs, markerPath(p), { kind, startedAt, seq });
  1081	   255	    ensureDir(io.fs, p.historyDir);
  1082	   256	
  1083	   257	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.snapshot)) {
  1084	   258	      // Atomic freeze (gate-2 minor 16): tmp+rename, so a crash mid-copy can
  1085	   259	      // never leave a partial history snapshot that recovery would trust.
  1086	   260	      atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.json`, io.fs.readFileSync(p.snapshot, 'utf8'));
  1087	   261	    } else {
  1088	   262	      atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.json`, '');
  1089	   263	    }
  1090	   264	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
  1091	   265	      io.fs.renameSync(p.journal, `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.ndjson`);
  1092	   266	    } else {
  1093	   267	      atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.historyDir}/${stem}.ndjson`, '');
  1094	   268	    }
  1095	   269	    atomicWriteText(io.fs, p.journal, '');
  1096	   270	
  1097	   271	    // Retention: newest RETAIN_PER_KIND per kind (ISO-derived stems sort by time).
  1098	   272	    const freezes = io.fs
  1099	   273	      .readdirSync(p.historyDir)
  1100	   274	      .filter((/** @type {string} */ n) => n.endsWith(`.${kind}.json`))
  1101	   275	      .sort();
  1102	   276	    while (freezes.length > RETAIN_PER_KIND) {
  1103	   277	      const oldest = /** @type {string} */ (freezes.shift());
  1104	   278	      const oldStem = oldest.slice(0, -'.json'.length);
  1105	   279	      for (const suffix of ['.json', '.ndjson']) {
  1106	   280	        try {
  1107	   281	          io.fs.unlinkSync(`${p.historyDir}/${oldStem}${suffix}`);
  1108	   282	        } catch {
  1109	   283	          // already absent — retention is best-effort per file
  1110	   284	        }
  1111	   285	      }
  1112	   286	    }
  1113	   287	
  1114	   288	    io.fs.unlinkSync(markerPath(p));
  1115	   289	    return stem;
  1116	   290	  });
  1117	   291	}

  1126	     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
  1127	     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
  1128	     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
  1129	     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
  1130	     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
  1131	     6	//
  1132	     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
  1133	     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
  1134	     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
  1135	    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
  1136	    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
  1137	    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
  1138	    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
  1139	    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
  1140	    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
  1141	    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
  1142	    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
  1143	    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
  1144	    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.

  1148	    23	export function makeIo({
  1149	    24	  files = {},
  1150	    25	  env = {},
  1151	    26	  stdin = '',
  1152	    27	  execResults = {},
  1153	    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
  1154	    29	  host = 'host-A',
  1155	    30	  pid = 4242,
  1156	    31	  startTime = 111000,
  1157	    32	  processAlive = null,
  1158	    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
  1159	    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
  1160	    35	} = {}) {
  1161	    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
  1162	    37	  const outChunks = [];
  1163	    38	  const errChunks = [];
  1164	    39	  let currentNow = now;
  1165	    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
  1166	    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
  1167	    42	
  1168	    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
  1169	    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
  1170	    45	
  1171	    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
  1172	    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
  1173	    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
  1174	    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
  1175	    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
  1176	    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
  1177	    52	        return reject(e);
  1178	    53	      }
  1179	    54	      const r = execResults[key];
  1180	    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
  1181	    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
  1182	    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
  1183	    58	      if (code !== 0) {
  1184	    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
  1185	    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
  1186	    61	        e.code = code;
  1187	    62	        e.stdout = out;
  1188	    63	        e.stderr = err;
  1189	    64	        return reject(e);
  1190	    65	      }
  1191	    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
  1192	    67	    });
  1193	    68	  };
  1194	    69	
  1195	    70	  return {
  1196	    71	    cwd: '/repo',
  1197	    72	    env,
  1198	    73	    stdin,
  1199	    74	    stdout,
  1200	    75	    stderr,
  1201	    76	    fs,
  1202	    77	    execFile,
  1203	    78	    now: () => currentNow,
  1204	    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
  1205	    80	    execPath,
  1206	    81	    host,
  1207	    82	    pid,
  1208	    83	    startTime,
  1209	    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
  1210	    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
  1211	    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
  1212	    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
  1213	    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
  1214	    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
  1215	    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
  1216	    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
  1217	    92	  };
  1218	    93	}

  1254	    34	/**

  1273	    53	
  1274	    54	// The bound token inputs, in the fixed order the structured token serializes
  1275	    55	// them. The human names power drift diagnosis: a stale-token rejection names
  1276	    56	// exactly which input(s) moved instead of listing six guesses.
  1277	    57	const TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
  1278	    58	  ['generation', 'bundle generation'],
  1279	    59	  ['journalSeq', 'journal seq'],
  1280	    60	  ['handoffStatus', 'handoff status'],
  1281	    61	  ['receives', 'receive count'],

  1283	    63	  ['reason', 'intake reason'],

  1286	    66	  ['gitDigest', 'git state'],
  1287	    67	  ['probesDigest', 'probe cache'],
  1288	    68	  ['platform', 'target platform'],
  1289	    69	  ['sessionHint', 'target session'],
  1290	    70	]);
  1291	    71	const TOKEN_PREFIX = 'rcpt1';
  1292	    72	
  1293	    73	/**
  1294	    74	 * The revision fingerprint the receipt token derives from. Every field is a
  1295	    75	 * bound input: any drift between prepare and commit re-derives a different
  1296	    76	 * token, so a stale receipt can never commit (plan §Concurrency).
  1297	    77	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
  1298	    78	 * @returns {Record<string, any>}
  1299	    79	 */
  1300	    80	function tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io) {
  1301	    81	  let configDigest = 'absent';
  1302	    82	  try {
  1303	    83	    configDigest = dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/baton.config.json`, 'utf8'));
  1304	    84	  } catch {
  1305	    85	    // no config — 'absent' is itself a bound value
  1306	    86	  }
  1307	    87	  return {
  1308	    88	    generation: bundle.generation,
  1309	    89	    journalSeq: bundle.journalSeq,
  1310	    90	    // The seal state is a bound input too (gate-2 fix): a competing receiver
  1311	    91	    // that lands between this token's derivation and its commit changes
  1312	    92	    // status/receive_log, so the loser's token cannot re-derive.
  1313	    93	    handoffStatus: bundle.handoff?.status ?? null,
  1314	    94	    receives: Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) ? bundle.handoff.receive_log.length : 0,
  1315	    95	    origin: opts.origin,
  1316	    96	    reason: opts.reason,
  1317	    97	    // Explicit --reason-class is a bound intake input (gate-2 iter-2 M4): it
  1318	    98	    // changes the avoid[] decision, so a change between prepare and commit is
  1319	    99	    // drift that must re-prepare.
  1320	   100	    reasonClass: opts.reasonClass ?? null,
  1321	   101	    configDigest,
  1322	   102	    gitDigest: dedupeKey(opts.gitSnapshot ?? null),
  1323	   103	    probesDigest: dedupeKey(opts.probes ?? null),
  1324	   104	    platform: opts.platform,
  1325	   105	    sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
  1326	   106	  };
  1327	   107	}
  1328	   108	
  1329	   109	/**
  1330	   110	 * Structured receipt token: a fixed-order join of per-input digests. Equality
  1331	   111	 * still gates commit exactly as the old opaque hash did; the structure only
  1332	   112	 * buys DIAGNOSIS — a mismatch can name the drifted input(s).
  1333	   113	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
  1334	   114	 */
  1335	   115	function deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io) {
  1336	   116	  const inputs = tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io);
  1337	   117	  return `${TOKEN_PREFIX}.` + TOKEN_FIELDS.map(([key]) => dedupeKey(inputs[key] ?? null).slice(0, 10)).join('.');
  1338	   118	}
  1339	   119	
  1340	   120	/**
  1341	   121	 * Name the inputs whose digests differ between two structured tokens.
  1342	   122	 * @param {string} presented @param {string} expected @returns {string[]}
  1343	   123	 */
  1344	   124	function driftedInputs(presented, expected) {
  1345	   125	  const a = presented.split('.');
  1346	   126	  const b = expected.split('.');
  1347	   127	  if (a[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || b[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || a.length !== b.length) return [];
  1348	   128	  return TOKEN_FIELDS.filter((_, i) => a[i + 1] !== b[i + 1]).map(([, name]) => name);
  1349	   129	}
  1350	   130	
  1351	   131	/**
  1352	   132	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
  1353	   133	 * @returns {string[]} staleness/degradation warnings
  1354	   134	 */
  1355	   135	function stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io) {
  1356	   136	  /** @type {string[]} */
  1357	   137	  const warnings = [];
  1358	   138	  if (bundle.handoff.status === 'open') {
  1359	   139	    warnings.push('source bundle is unsealed (open) — the handoff was never finalized; treating this as a degraded (limit-death) receive');
  1360	   140	  }
  1361	   141	  const ref = bundle.handoff.finalizedAt ?? bundle.updatedAt;
  1362	   142	  const ageMs = Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(ref);
  1363	   143	  if (Number.isFinite(ageMs) && ageMs > STALE_AFTER_MS) {
  1364	   144	    warnings.push(`bundle finalized ${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h ago (staleness threshold 12h) — re-verify before continuing`);
  1365	   145	  }
  1366	   146	  const g = opts.gitSnapshot;
  1367	   147	  if (g && bundle.git) {
  1368	   148	    if (bundle.git.headSha !== g.headSha) {
  1369	   149	      warnings.push(`HEAD moved since capture: bundle recorded ${bundle.git.headSha}, working tree now ${g.headSha}`);
  1370	   150	    }
  1371	   151	    if (bundle.git.dirty !== g.dirty) {
  1372	   152	      warnings.push(`dirty-state mismatch: bundle captured dirty=${bundle.git.dirty}, working tree now dirty=${g.dirty}`);
  1373	   153	    }
  1374	   154	  }
  1375	   155	  return warnings;
  1376	   156	}
  1377	   157	
  1378	   158	/**
  1379	   159	 * Phase 1 of the receive transaction: validate, resolve roles, render the
  1380	   160	 * resume prompt, and return a one-time receipt token. Mutates NOTHING — the
  1381	   161	 * token is self-contained (a fingerprint hash), never a persisted record.
  1382	   162	 * @param {string} root @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
  1383	   163	 * @returns {{token: string, prompt: string, assignments: Record<string, any>, warnings: string[]}}
  1384	   164	 */
  1385	   165	export function prepare(root, opts, io) {
  1386	   166	  const { bundle, warnings: loadWarnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
  1387	   167	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive`);
  1388	   168	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
  1389	   169	
  1390	   170	  const warnings = [...loadWarnings, ...stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io)];
  1391	   171	
  1392	   172	  const { config, errors } = loadConfig(root, io);
  1393	   173	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
  1394	   174	  let assignments = {};
  1395	   175	  if (config) {
  1396	   176	    // The dead origin comes from the BUNDLE first (its sealed reasonClass and
  1397	   177	    // recorded origin platform); typed intake only fills the gaps — a user
  1398	   178	    // should not need to retype the verbatim limit string to keep roles off
  1399	   179	    // the platform that died (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 4). Confidence gates
  1400	   180	    // auto-avoidance (gate-2 iter-2 M4): an explicit --reason-class or a sealed
  1401	   181	    // reasonClass is trusted; a live text classification only auto-avoids at
  1402	   182	    // medium+ confidence, else it warns and defers to explicit intake.
  1403	   183	    let reasonClass;
  1404	   184	    let confidence;
  1405	   185	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
  1406	   186	      reasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
  1407	   187	      confidence = 'high'; // explicit user intake
  1408	   188	    } else if (typeof bundle.handoff?.reasonClass === 'string') {
  1409	   189	      reasonClass = bundle.handoff.reasonClass;
  1410	   190	      confidence = 'high'; // trusted seal
  1411	   191	    } else {
  1412	   192	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
  1413	   193	      reasonClass = v.class;
  1414	   194	      confidence = v.confidence;
  1415	   195	    }
  1416	   196	    const deadOrigin =
  1417	   197	      typeof bundle.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : opts.origin;
  1418	   198	    /** @type {string[]} */
  1419	   199	    let avoid = [];
  1420	   200	    if (reasonClass === 'usage-limit') {
  1421	   201	      if (confidence === 'low') {
  1422	   202	        warnings.push(
  1423	   203	          `the switch reason matched a usage-limit heuristic only at LOW confidence — not auto-avoiding ${deadOrigin}; pass --reason-class usage-limit to confirm the failover away from it`,
  1424	   204	        );
  1425	   205	      } else {
  1426	   206	        avoid = [deadOrigin];
  1427	   207	      }
  1428	   208	    }
  1429	   209	    assignments = resolveRoles({ config, to: opts.platform, avoid, nativeOnly: false, probes: opts.probes ?? null }).assignments;
  1430	   210	  } else {
  1431	   211	    warnings.push(`role matrix unavailable (${errors.map((e) => e.msg).join('; ')}) — no role table in this prompt`);
  1432	   212	  }
  1433	   213	
  1434	   214	  const prompt = renderResumePrompt({ bundle, assignments, warnings, origin: opts.origin, reason: opts.reason });
  1435	   215	  return { token: deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io), prompt, assignments, warnings };
  1436	   216	}
  1437	   217	
  1438	   218	/**
  1439	   219	 * Phase 2: one atomic transition. Re-derives every bound input from current
  1440	   220	 * disk state + opts and rejects ANY drift (throws, zero mutation). On match:
  1441	   221	 * writes the received seal, archives it via a 'receive' rotation, then opens a
  1442	   222	 * fresh writable generation owned by the receiving platform/session.
  1443	   223	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
  1444	   224	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
  1445	   225	 */
  1446	   226	export function commit(root, token, opts, io) {
  1447	   227	  // ONE operation-scoped lock across revalidation and every mutation (gate-2
  1448	   228	  // fix: reviewers a#1/b#11): two competing receivers serialize here, and the
  1449	   229	  // loser's re-derivation sees the winner's generation/status/receive_log.
  1450	   230	  return withLock(root, io, (fence) => commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence));
  1451	   231	}
  1452	   232	
  1453	   233	/**
  1454	   234	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io @param {string} fence
  1455	   235	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
  1456	   236	 */
  1457	   237	function commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence) {
  1458	   238	  const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
  1459	   239	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive; run receive --prepare first`);
  1460	   240	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
  1461	   241	
  1462	   242	  const expected = deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io);
  1463	   243	  if (expected !== token) {
  1464	   244	    // Idempotency first (audit finding: duplicate-receive corruption): if THIS
  1465	   245	    // exact receipt already landed — its digest is recorded on a receive_log
  1466	   246	    // entry — the retry is a lost-output re-send, not drift. Report success
  1467	   247	    // with zero mutation instead of sending the model into a re-prepare +
  1468	   248	    // re-commit loop that executes a second full receive.
  1469	   249	    const receipt = dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16);
  1470	   250	    const landed = Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) && bundle.handoff.receive_log.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => e?.receipt === receipt);
  1471	   251	    if (landed) {
  1472	   252	      return { adopted: false, alreadyCommitted: true, generation: bundle.generation, archivedTo: null };
  1473	   253	    }
  1474	   254	    const drifted = driftedInputs(token, expected);
  1475	   255	    const detail = drifted.length > 0 ? `drifted input(s): ${drifted.join(', ')}` : 'a bound input drifted since prepare';
  1476	   256	    throw new Error(
  1477	   257	      `receipt token is stale — ${detail}. Re-prepare and retry, passing the SAME intake flags (--origin/--reason/--reason-class) to both prepare and --commit`,
  1478	   258	    );
  1479	   259	  }
  1480	   260	
  1481	   261	  const degraded = bundle.handoff.status !== 'sealed';
  1482	   262	  const receiveEntry = {
  1483	   263	    origin: opts.origin,
  1484	   264	    reason: opts.reason,
  1485	   265	    at: io.now(),
  1486	   266	    // The receipt digest keys the idempotent-retry detection above.
  1487	   267	    receipt: dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16),
  1488	   268	    ...(degraded ? { degradedSeal: true } : {}),
  1489	   269	  };
  1490	   270	
  1491	   271	  // The degraded seal must NOT persist a LOW-confidence classification (iter-3
  1492	   272	  // F10): a sealed reasonClass is trusted as high-confidence by the next
  1493	   273	  // prepare, so laundering a low-confidence heuristic here would silently
  1494	   274	  // auto-avoid the origin one hop later. Explicit intake is high-confidence and
  1495	   275	  // seals; a live classification seals ONLY at medium+ confidence, else null
  1496	   276	  // (the next receive re-classifies the reason text at its true confidence).
  1497	   277	  let sealedReasonClass = null;
  1498	   278	  if (degraded) {
  1499	   279	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
  1500	   280	      sealedReasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
  1501	   281	    } else {
  1502	   282	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
  1503	   283	      sealedReasonClass = v.confidence === 'low' ? null : v.class;
  1504	   284	    }
  1505	   285	  }
  1506	   286	
  1507	   287	  const receivedSeal = {
  1508	   288	    ...bundle,
  1509	   289	    updatedAt: io.now(),
  1510	   290	    handoff: {
  1511	   291	      ...bundle.handoff,
  1512	   292	      status: 'received',
  1513	   293	      ...(degraded
  1514	   294	        ? {
  1515	   295	            reason: opts.reason,
  1516	   296	            reasonClass: sealedReasonClass,
  1517	   297	            finalizedAt: io.now(),
  1518	   298	          }
  1519	   299	        : {}),
  1520	   300	      receive_log: [...bundle.handoff.receive_log, receiveEntry],
  1521	   301	    },
  1522	   302	  };
  1523	   303	
  1524	   304	  // Freeze order: the received seal is written first so the 'receive' rotation
  1525	   305	  // archives exactly that state (never the pre-commit bundle). All three
  1526	   306	  // mutations run under the SAME lock acquisition, fence-checked per write.
  1527	   307	  writeSnapshotIn(root, receivedSeal, io, fence);
  1528	   308	  const archivedTo = rotateJournalIn(root, 'receive', io, fence);
  1529	   309	
  1530	   310	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
  1531	   311	  const next = {
  1532	   312	    ...receivedSeal,
  1533	   313	    generation: bundle.generation + 1,
  1534	   314	    origin: {
  1535	   315	      platform: opts.platform,
  1536	   316	      model: config?.defaults?.[opts.platform] ?? 'unknown',
  1537	   317	      sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
  1538	   318	      unstable: opts.sessionUnstable === true,
  1539	   319	    },
  1540	   320	    // The fresh writable generation has NO switch reason yet (iter-4 I3): the
  1541	   321	    // sealed reason/reasonClass/finalizedAt/toPlatformHint describe the PRIOR
  1542	   322	    // handoff, not this one. Carrying them forward made the next prepare trust a
  1543	   323	    // stale class as a high-confidence seal (silently auto-avoiding the healthy
  1544	   324	    // adopted origin) and poisoned both the 12 h staleness reference and the
  1545	   325	    // sessionStart limit-hit signal. Keep only receive_log (the audit chain).
  1546	   326	    handoff: { ...receivedSeal.handoff, status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, finalizedAt: null, toPlatformHint: null },
  1547	   327	  };
  1548	   328	  writeSnapshotIn(root, next, io, fence);
  1549	   329	
  1550	   330	  return { adopted: true, generation: next.generation, archivedTo };
  1551	   331	}

  1602	    47	
  1603	    48	/**
  1604	    49	 * Validate a loop spec against the role matrix. Reports EVERY problem at
  1605	    50	 * once (each error names its offender) so a spec author fixes one round, not
  1606	    51	 * one field per run. Valid specs are returned normalized: caller-provided
  1607	    52	 * fields are preserved verbatim; only ABSENT constraints/budgets/smoke are
  1608	    53	 * filled with defaults.
  1609	    54	 * @param {any} spec
  1610	    55	 * @param {any} config the parsed baton.config.json (roles are checked against it)
  1611	    56	 * @returns {{ok: true, spec: any} | {ok: false, errors: string[]}}
  1612	    57	 */
  1613	    58	export function validateLoopSpec(spec, config) {
  1614	    59	  /** @type {string[]} */
  1615	    60	  const errors = [];
  1616	    61	  const s = spec ?? {};
  1617	    62	
  1618	    63	  if (s.schema !== LOOP_SCHEMA) errors.push(`schema must be '${LOOP_SCHEMA}' (got ${JSON.stringify(s.schema)})`);
  1619	    64	  if (typeof s.goal !== 'string' || s.goal.length === 0) errors.push('goal must be a non-empty string');
  1620	    65	
  1621	    66	  let constraints = s.constraints;
  1622	    67	  if (constraints === undefined) constraints = [];
  1623	    68	  if (!Array.isArray(constraints)) {
  1624	    69	    errors.push('constraints must be an array of strings');
  1625	    70	  } else {
  1626	    71	    constraints.forEach((c, i) => {
  1627	    72	      if (typeof c !== 'string') errors.push(`constraints[${i}] must be a string (got ${JSON.stringify(c)})`);
  1628	    73	    });
  1629	    74	  }
  1630	    75	
  1631	    76	  const roles = config?.roles ?? {};
  1632	    77	  if (!Array.isArray(s.phases) || s.phases.length === 0) {
  1633	    78	    errors.push('phases must be a non-empty array of {id, role}');
  1634	    79	  } else {
  1635	    80	    s.phases.forEach((/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {number} */ i) => {
  1636	    81	      if (typeof p?.id !== 'string' || p.id.length === 0) errors.push(`phases[${i}] is missing an id (a non-empty progress label)`);
  1637	    82	      if (typeof p?.role !== 'string' || p.role.length === 0) {
  1638	    83	        errors.push(`phases[${i}] is missing a role (a baton.config.json role ID)`);
  1639	    84	      } else if (!(p.role in roles)) {
  1640	    85	        errors.push(`phases[${i}]: unknown role '${p.role}' — add it to baton.config.json roles or fix the reference`);
  1641	    86	      }
  1642	    87	    });
  1643	    88	  }
  1644	    89	
  1645	    90	  let budgets = s.budgets;
  1646	    91	  if (budgets === undefined) {
  1647	    92	    budgets = { ...DEFAULT_BUDGETS };
  1648	    93	  } else if (typeof budgets !== 'object' || budgets === null) {
  1649	    94	    errors.push('budgets must be an object');
  1650	    95	  } else {
  1651	    96	    const cap = budgets.iterationCap;
  1652	    97	    if (typeof cap !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(cap) || cap < 1 || cap > ITERATION_CAP_MAX) {
  1653	    98	      errors.push(`budgets.iterationCap must be a number between 1 and ${ITERATION_CAP_MAX} (the 5-iteration cap is a hard invariant; got ${JSON.stringify(cap)})`);
  1654	    99	    }
  1655	   100	    for (const field of ['perRoleTimeoutMin', 'maxChildrenPerPhase']) {
  1656	   101	      const v = budgets[field];
  1657	   102	      if (typeof v !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(v) || v <= 0) {
  1658	   103	        errors.push(`budgets.${field} must be a positive number (got ${JSON.stringify(v)})`);
  1659	   104	      }
  1660	   105	    }
  1661	   106	  }
  1662	   107	
  1663	   108	  let smoke = s.smoke;
  1664	   109	  if (smoke === undefined) {
  1665	   110	    smoke = { cmd: null, expect: null };
  1666	   111	  } else if (typeof smoke !== 'object' || smoke === null) {
  1667	   112	    errors.push('smoke must be an object ({cmd, expect}, each a string or null)');
  1668	   113	  } else {
  1669	   114	    if (!(smoke.cmd === null || typeof smoke.cmd === 'string')) errors.push(`smoke.cmd must be a string or null (got ${JSON.stringify(smoke.cmd)})`);
  1670	   115	    if (!(smoke.expect === null || typeof smoke.expect === 'string')) errors.push(`smoke.expect must be a string or null (got ${JSON.stringify(smoke.expect)})`);
  1671	   116	  }
  1672	   117	
  1673	   118	  if (errors.length > 0) return { ok: false, errors };
  1674	   119	  return { ok: true, spec: { ...s, constraints, budgets, smoke } };
  1675	   120	}
